That's really when these feelings started getting so bad.\n\nDoctor: Over this past month, have you noticed a loss of interest or pleasure in things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yes, I have. Um, I haven’t even touched my camera lately. I haven't checked the bird feeders with any real interest, or... or even cared about what’s coming up in the garden. It's actually pretty frightening to me, because doing those things... that used to be how I felt like myself.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period lasting several days or more where you felt unusually energetic, elated, or needed much less sleep than normal, along with racing thoughts or impulsive behavior?\n\nPatient: Well, yes, actually. Earlier this month... I had a few days where I felt almost electrically upbeat. But then I would just snap at everyone over really tiny things. Like at work, if a medication cup was misplaced, or if there was a sudden change in the morning schedule, I would get so angry. It really didn't feel like my usual self to react that way.\n\nDoctor: During that period when you felt unusually energetic and irritable, how many days in a row did that last?\n\nPatient: Um, I'm not exactly sure, to be honest. I didn't really keep track of how many days it was in a row. Everything has just been such a blur lately with all the stress at home... so I really couldn't give you an exact number.\n\nDoctor: During those few days when you felt unusually energetic and irritable, did you notice you needed much less sleep than usual but still felt rested and full of energy?\n\nPatient: Yes, definitely. During that high-energy stretch, I was sleeping maybe three hours a night. I'd still get up before sunrise feeling completely sharp and ready to go. I just felt like I didn't really need any more rest than that.\n\nDoctor: Thinking back, do you remember if that unusually energetic, irritable stretch lasted at least four days in a row, or was it shorter than that?\n\nPatient: I... I honestly couldn't tell you for sure. I know you're looking for a specific number, but like I said, it's all just been such a blur. It might have been four days, or maybe it was just three. I really didn't track it closely enough to say definitely one way or the other.\n\nDoctor: During that energetic, irritable stretch, did anyone else—like coworkers or family—comment that you seemed different or unusually keyed up?\n\nPatient: No, not that I can remember. I mean, my husband might have said I was a little snappy, but nobody at the clinic said anything about me acting different or being keyed up. If they noticed, they didn't mention it to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period like this before—weeks of low mood, low energy, and loss of interest—at any other time in your life prior to this past month?\n\nPatient: Yes. Actually, it hasn't just been this past month. The... well, the depression has been hanging over me for about 11 months now. This past month is when it made everything feel completely unbearable, but yes, it started way before that.\n\nDoctor: Can you recall if that high-energy, irritable stretch spanned parts of a single work week, like from one shift to another, or do you remember it crossing into a new week?\n\nPatient: I really don't know. Like I was saying before, I just didn't keep track of the days like that. I can't remember if it was in the middle of a week or if it carried over the weekend into the next one. It's just... it's hard to separate the days out when you're looking back at it.\n\nDoctor: During that high-energy, irritable stretch, did you do anything unusually impulsive or out of character—like impulsive spending, rash decisions, or taking risks you normally wouldn't take?\n\nPatient: Yes, I did. I ended up buying a bunch of stuff for the clinic that we didn't strictly need... well, I thought we did at the time. I bought monitoring equipment, some new ramps, bedding, just a lot of supplies on impulse. It all felt urgently necessary right then, like I had to get it immediately. But afterward, I realized I'd put all these charges on the credit card that we really can't cover. It was definitely out of character for me to spend like that without checking our budget first.\n\nDoctor: Do you remember if that high-energy, irritable period was still going on the next time you had a day off from work, or had it already ended by then?\n\nPatient: I really can't say for sure. Like I mentioned, the days all just kind of blur together when I try to think back on it. I don't remember if it lasted until my next day off or if it had stopped before then. Everything has just been such a blur lately with all the stress at home... so I really couldn't give you an exact number.\n\nDoctor: During those few days when you felt unusually energetic and irritable, did you notice you needed much less sleep than usual but still felt rested and full of energy?\n\nPatient: Yes, definitely. During that high-energy stretch, I was sleeping maybe three hours a night. I'd still get up before sunrise feeling completely sharp and ready to go. I just felt like I didn't really need any more rest than that.\n\nDoctor: Thinking back, do you remember if that unusually energetic, irritable stretch lasted at least four days in a row, or was it shorter than that?\n\nPatient: I... I honestly couldn't tell you for sure. I know you're looking for a specific number, but like I said, it's all just been such a blur. It might have been four days, or maybe it was just three. I really didn't track it closely enough to say definitely one way or the other.\n\nDoctor: During that energetic, irritable stretch, did anyone else—like coworkers or family—comment that you seemed different or unusually keyed up?\n\nPatient: No, not that I can remember. I mean, my husband might have said I was a little snappy, but nobody at the clinic said anything about me acting different or being keyed up. Like I was saying before, I just didn't keep track of the days like that. I can't remember if it was in the middle of a week or if it carried over the weekend into the next one. It's just... it's hard to separate the days out when you're looking back at it.\n\nDoctor: During that high-energy, irritable stretch, did you do anything unusually impulsive or out of character—like impulsive spending, rash decisions, or taking risks you normally wouldn't take?\n\nPatient: Yes, I did. I ended up buying a bunch of stuff for the clinic that we didn't strictly need... well, I thought we did at the time. I bought monitoring equipment, some new ramps, bedding, just a lot of supplies on impulse. It all felt urgently necessary right then, like I had to get it immediately. But afterward, I realized I'd put all these charges on the credit card that we really can't cover. It was definitely out of character for me to spend like that without checking our budget first.\n\nDoctor: Do you remember if that high-energy, irritable period was still going on the next time you had a day off from work, or had it already ended by then?\n\nPatient: I really can't say for sure. Like I mentioned, the days all just kind of blur together when I try to think back on it. I don't remember if it lasted until my next day off or if it had stopped before then. I just wasn't paying attention to the timeline like that.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me how many days passed between when you first started feeling that high-energy, irritable way and when you made that big purchase of clinic supplies?\n\nPatient: I honestly don't know how many days it was. I just remember feeling like I had to do it right then, but the exact timeline... I just don't remember if the low mood lifted completely or not.\n\nDoctor: \n\nPatient: I'm not sure. I don't really have an answer for that.
